WSK Mielec|MiG-15||1527|Fagot||fixed_wing|monoplane|military|fighter|1956|COMPOSITE. Built at WSK Mielec in April 1956 as Lim-2 works number 1B 15-027 (tactical 1527). Served 29. PLM Orneta from 24 April 1956; Bydgoszcz 1960-1963; rebuilt to Lim-2R reconnaissance standard at LZR nr 4 Warszawa in 1964; 21. PLRT Sochaczew from 10 June 1964; WSL Dęblin by 1972; then COSSTWL Oleśnica. Given to the town in December 1976 as a gift of the Polish People's Air Force and mounted on a steel frame built by the Andoria engine works. A 2015 survey at the Polish Aviation Museum in Kraków established that only the forward fuselage is 1B 15-027; the rear fuselage and wings come from MiG-15/Lim-1 airframes of the 1A series|MiG15;Lim-2;Lim2;Lim-2R;Fagot;c/n 1B 15-027|on_display|PL|Samolot Lim-2 w Andrychowie
Mikoyan-Gurevich|MiG-21|F||Fishbed-B||fixed_wing|monoplane|military|fighter||Ex-Soviet airframe donated by the Soviet garrison at Szprotawa-Wiechlice and unveiled 9 May 1985 as a monument to the 40th anniversary of the victory over fascism; the plaque was cast by Dolnośląskie Zakłady Odlewnicze on the initiative of the local LOK branch. Repainted in Polish Air Force markings after 1989 but Soviet red stars remain visible under the paint on the fin — the Polish markings it now wears are NOT its identity. Local sources call it a MiG-21F rather than an F-13 on the basis of its twin NR-30 cannon; unverified on site|MiG21;Fishbed;MiG-21 typ 72|on_display|RU|MiG-21F Victory Monument -- Szprotawa
